{{ElectionsTX}}

The '''2026 United States Senate election in Texas''' will be held on November 3, 2026, to elect a member of the [[United States Senate]] to represent the state of [[Texas]]. [[Republican Party (United States)|Republican]] state attorney general [[Ken Paxton]] and [[Democratic Party (United States)|Democratic]] state representative [[James Talarico]] are the nominees for their respective parties.

Four-term Republican incumbent [[John Cornyn]] sought re-election, but was challenged for the party's nomination by Paxton. After no candidate received a majority in the March 3 primary, Paxton, aided by a late endorsement from President [[Donald Trump]], defeated Cornyn with 63.8% of the vote in a [[two-round system|runoff]] on May 26. Cornyn became the first incumbent senator from Texas to lose renomination since Democrat [[Ralph Yarborough]] in [[1970 United States Senate election in Texas|1970]]. In the Democratic primary, Talarico won the nomination with 52.4% of the vote over congresswoman [[Jasmine Crockett]].

Democrats have not won a Senate election in Texas since [[1988 United States Senate election in Texas|1988]].

{{toclimit|4}}

== Background ==
Texas has been considered a [[Republican Party (United States)|Republican]]-leaning state throughout the 21st century.<ref>{{Cite web |title=How Texas came to be dominated by Republicans |url=https://www.axios.com/local/dallas/2024/09/05/texas-republican-party-dominance |last=Rajwani-Dharsi |first=Naheed |website=[[Axios (website)|Axios]] |date=September 5, 2024 |access-date=August 19, 2026 |language=en}}</ref> Democrats have not won a [[U.S. Senate]] election in the state since [[Lloyd Bentsen]] was re-elected in [[1988 United States Senate election in Texas|1988]], and they have not won any statewide election in the state since [[Lieutenant Governor of Texas|lieutenant governor]] [[Bob Bullock]] was re-elected in [[1994 Texas lieutenant gubernatorial election|1994]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Staff |first=FOX 4 |date=October 30, 2024 |title=No Democrat has won a statewide office in Texas since 1994 |url=https://www.fox4news.com/election/texas-democrat-statewide-senate-president |access-date=August 19, 2026 |website=FOX 4 |language=en-US}}</ref> Republicans have fully controlled the [[Texas Legislature]] since the [[2002 Texas House of Representatives election|2002 elections]]. Texas's Republican lean has waned in the past decade, with Democrats only narrowly losing the 2018 elections for [[2018 United States Senate election in Texas|U.S. Senate]] and [[2018 Texas Attorney General election|attorney general]], buoyed by gains in the state's suburban areas. More recently, however, Republicans have made substantial gains with the state's [[Hispanic and Latino Americans|Hispanic]] population, particularly in the [[Rio Grande Valley]]. Republican [[Donald Trump]] won the state by nearly 14 [[percentage point]]s in the [[2024 United States presidential election in Texas|2024 presidential election]], while U.S. Senator [[Ted Cruz]] [[2024 United States Senate election in Texas|won re-election]] by over 8 points on the same ballot.{{Citation needed|date=August 2026}}

Incumbent Republican senator [[John Cornyn]] was first elected in [[2002 United States Senate election in Texas|2002]] to succeed fellow Republican [[Phil Gramm]].<ref>{{Cite news |title=National Briefing {{!}} Southwest: Texas: New Senator Sworn In |url=https://timesmachine.nytimes.com/timesmachine/2002/12/03/204200.html |access-date=August 19, 2026 |work=The New York Times |language=en |issn=0362-4331}}</ref> Cornyn had previously served as the state's [[Texas Attorney General|attorney general]], as well as a member of the state's [[Supreme Court of Texas|Supreme Court]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Guo |first=Kayla |date=May 27, 2026 |title=John Cornyn's long political career ends in MAGA uprising |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/05/26/john-cornyn-texas-senator-paxton-maga-loss-legacy/ |access-date=August 19, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> He most recently [[2020 United States Senate election in Texas|won re-election in 2020]] with 53.5% of the vote against Democrat [[M. J. Hegar]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Svitek |first=Patrick |date=November 4, 2020 |title=John Cornyn defeats MJ Hegar to retain Texas' U.S. Senate seat |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2020/11/03/john-cornyn-mj-hegar-texas-senate/ |access-date=August 19, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> Cornyn has a consistently [[Conservatism|conservative]] voting record in the U.S. Senate, and he is considered part of the Republican establishment due to his long tenure in politics.<ref>{{Cite web |title=The Texas GOP finally turned on Cornyn |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2026/05/26/cornyn-senate-career-ends-trump-paxton-00937548 |access-date=August 19, 2026 |website=Politico |language=en}}</ref>

==Republican primary==
[[File:John Cornyn (cropped).jpg|thumb|x170px|Incumbent senator [[John Cornyn]] was defeated in the Republican primary runoff.]]
[[File:Wesley Hunt, official portrait, 118th Congress (cropped).jpg|thumb|x170px|[[U.S. Representative]] [[Wesley Hunt]] finished third in the Republican primary.]]

===Background===
Incumbent senator [[John Cornyn]], who was seeking a fifth term, faced a competitive primary from [[Texas Attorney General|attorney general]] [[Ken Paxton]] which progressed to a runoff after no candidate reached a majority in the first round. The two had clashed publicly since 2022, with Paxton criticizing Cornyn for being insufficiently conservative, particularly over his support for [[Ukraine–United States relations#Russo-Ukrainian War (2014–present)|U.S. aid to Ukraine]], [[DREAM Act]] for [[Deferred Action for Childhood Arrivals|DACA]] beneficiaries and playing a leading role in the passage of the [[Bipartisan Safer Communities Act|bipartisan gun safety bill]] after the [[Uvalde school massacre]]. Cornyn, in turn, has highlighted [[Ken Paxton#Legal issues|Paxton's legal troubles]], perceived character flaws and corruption, and his [[Ken Paxton#Impeachment|2023 impeachment]], in which Paxton was impeached by the Republican-controlled [[Texas House of Representatives|Texas House]] before the [[Texas Senate|Senate]] later acquitted him of all charges.<ref name="CP"/><ref>{{Cite web |last=Jacobs |first=Ben |date=May 14, 2025 |title=The Republican Trainwreck of the 2026 Election Cycle |url=https://www.politico.com/news/magazine/2025/05/14/cornyn-paxton-texas-gop-primary-infighting-00346390 |access-date=May 15, 2025 |website=Politico |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=McLaughlin |first=Seth |title=Sen. Cornyn's reelection bid faces more warning signs in new Senate primary poll |url=https://www.washingtontimes.com/news/2025/jun/3/sen-john-cornyns-reelection-bid-faces-warning-signs-new-senate/ |access-date=June 5, 2025 |website=The Washington Times |date=June 3, 2025 |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Goodman |first=J. David |date=May 30, 2025 |title=Cornyn Calls Primary Fight Against Ken Paxton a 'Test of Character' |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2025/05/30/us/cornyn-paxton-texas-primary.html |access-date=June 29, 2025 |work=The New York Times |language=en-US |issn=0362-4331}}</ref>

The race has been seen as a key contest between the [[Republican Party of Texas|Texas Republican Party]]'s establishment wing and its hardline conservative faction.<ref name="CP">{{Cite news |last=Gans |first=Jared |date=February 9, 2025 |title=7 competitive primaries to watch in 2026 |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5133732-2026-midterm-elections-primaries/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250210030210/https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5133732-2026-midterm-elections-primaries/ |archive-date=February 10, 2025 |access-date=February 13, 2025 |work=The Hill |language=en-US}}</ref> In recent years, insurgent [[Radical right (United States)|hard-right]] candidates have steadily gained ground, often ousting more traditional GOP officials. Polling reflects this divide: Paxton holds a commanding lead among "[[Trumpism|Trump Movement]]" voters, while Cornyn performs better with the smaller group of "[[Reagan coalition|Traditional Republicans]]."<ref>{{Cite web |last=Jacobs |first=Ben |date=June 7, 2025 |title='Trump movement' turns on Cornyn, poll finds |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2025/06/07/john-cornyn-ken-paxton-poll-trump-movement-00393045 |access-date=June 7, 2025 |website=Politico |language=en}}</ref> On May 19, 2026, [[Donald Trump]] announced his endorsement for Paxton,<ref>{{Cite web |last=McGraw |first=Elizabeth Findell and Meridith |date=May 19, 2026 |title=Trump Endorses Ken Paxton in Tight Texas GOP Senate Race |url=https://www.wsj.com/politics/elections/trump-texas-senate-endorsement-cornyn-paxton-8e7af62d |access-date=May 19, 2026 |website=The Wall Street Journal |language=en-US}}</ref> while previously describing both Paxton and Cornyn as good friends.<ref>{{Cite web |date=April 13, 2025 |title=Trump weighs in on Texas Senate race |url=https://www.wfaa.com/article/news/politics/trump-texas-senate-race-john-cornyn-ken-paxton/287-50981ce0-b6cb-4e88-a087-4f343710d4de |access-date=April 14, 2025 |website=WFAA |language=en-US}}</ref> His influence was widely seen as a key factor in determining the primary winner.<ref>{{Cite web |date=April 25, 2025 |title=There's a 'Nasty' Senate Primary Battle Brewing in Texas |url=https://www.notus.org/2026-election/texas-senate-primary-cornyn-paxton |access-date=April 25, 2025 |website=NOTUS |language=en-US}}</ref> He had previously criticized Cornyn as a "hopeless" [[RINO]] (Republican in name only) for supporting the Bipartisan Safer Communities Act, an attack Paxton's campaign highlighted in their ads.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Isenstadt |first=Alex |date=May 2, 2025 |title=Scoop: Pro-Paxton group courts Trump with anti-Cornyn ad |url=https://www.axios.com/2025/05/02/trump-ad-paxton-cornyn-texas-senate |access-date=May 2, 2025 |website=Axios |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |title=What becomes of Republicans who cross King Donald? |url=https://www.economist.com/united-states/2025/07/06/what-becomes-of-republicans-who-cross-king-donald |access-date=July 8, 2025 |newspaper=The Economist |issn=0013-0613}}</ref> [[Ted Cruz]], the junior senator, avoided taking a side in the primary, though he previously endorsed Cornyn in [[2020 United States Senate election in Texas|2020]].<ref>{{Cite web |first1=Abby |last1=Livingston |first2=Patrick |last2=Svitek |date=December 7, 2018 |title=Ted Cruz endorses John Cornyn for re-election |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2018/12/07/cruz-cornyn-endorsement/ |access-date=June 9, 2025 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en}}</ref>

The election takes place amid efforts by the state Republican Party to restrict its primary to registered members. Legislation to allow closed primaries failed, prompting the party to sue the state, claiming the current law violates the [[First Amendment to the United States Constitution|First Amendment]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Schneider |first=Andrew |date=December 29, 2025 |title=Texas GOP faces legal opposition in bid to close state's open primary system |url=https://www.keranews.org/politics/2025-12-29/texas-gop-faces-legal-opposition-in-bid-to-close-states-open-primary-system |access-date=January 15, 2026 |website=KERA News |language=en}}</ref> Despite serving as the state's attorney general, Paxton declined to defend the law in court, drawing criticism from those who argued he would gain electorally from a closed primary.<ref>{{Cite web |last=McCormick |first=Cara Brown |date=October 13, 2025 |title=Paxton Concedes He Has a Conflict of Interest in Texas Primary Lawsuit |url=https://ivn.us/posts/paxton-concedes-he-has-conflict-interest-texas-primary-lawsuit-2025-10-13 |access-date=January 15, 2026 |website=Independent Voter News}}</ref>

=== First round ===

==== Campaign ====
Paxton led Cornyn in early polling both before and after entering the race, though concerns about his general election viability persisted, as polls showed him underperforming Cornyn and, in some cases, trailing hypothetical Democratic opponents.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Gans |first=Jared |date=May 12, 2025 |title=Cornyn losing to primary rival in poll from top GOP super PAC |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5295745-texas-gop-primary-cornyn-paxton/ |access-date=May 14, 2025 |website=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]]}}</ref> This led many Republicans to back Cornyn, as Senate GOP leaders pressed Trump for an endorsement.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Everett |first=Burgess |date=February 4, 2026 |title=Republicans fear a Texas Senate problem — and hope Trump agrees |url=https://www.semafor.com/article/02/04/2026/republicans-wonder-if-trump-is-listening-to-their-texas-senate-worries |access-date=February 5, 2026 |website=Semafor}}</ref> Cornyn told ''[[The Wall Street Journal]]'' in June 2025 that he would consider stepping aside if a stronger candidate emerged to defeat Paxton, but he quickly walked back the comment, stating he would remain in the race.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Doud |first=Justin |date=June 27, 2025 |title=U.S. Sen. John Cornyn rebukes report that he's considering dropping out of 2026 race |url=https://www.houstonpublicmedia.org/articles/news/politics/2025/06/27/525205/u-s-sen-john-cornyn-rebukes-report-that-hes-considering-dropping-out-of-2026-race/ |access-date=June 29, 2025 |website=[[KUHT]] |language=en-US}}</ref> Congressman [[Wesley Hunt]] entered the race in late 2025, further scrambling the race and increasing the chance of a runoff.<ref>{{Cite web |last1=Haake |first1=Garrett |last2=Bowman |first2=Bridget |date=November 25, 2025 |title='Only begun to fight': Texas Senate race heats up as Republicans brace for a runoff |url=https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/texas-senate-race-republicans-brace-runoff-rcna245613 |access-date=January 12, 2026 |website=NBC News |language=en}}</ref>

All three candidates competed for the endorsement of Donald Trump, having made support for him and his agenda central to their campaigns; however, Trump declined to back a single candidate, instead stating he supported "all three".<ref>{{Cite web |last=Wallace |first=Jeremy |date=January 10, 2026 |title=Donald Trump tells John Cornyn he's still not ready to endorse in Senate primary |url=https://www.houstonchronicle.com/politics/texas-take/article/john-cornyn-donald-trump-endorse-texas-senate-21286442.php |access-date=January 12, 2026 |website=[[Houston Chronicle]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Brams |first=Sophie |date=February 17, 2026 |title=Trump says he hasn't decided endorsement in Texas Senate race |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5741247-donald-trump-texas-senate-primary/ |access-date=February 17, 2026 |website=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]]}}</ref> Cornyn had maintained a substantial fundraising lead over the other candidates throughout the race, spending much of it attacking Paxton.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Crampton |first=Liz |date=January 12, 2026 |title=Cornyn rakes in record fundraising ahead of Texas primary |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2026/01/12/cornyn-rakes-in-record-fundraising-ahead-of-texas-primary-00721699 |access-date=January 12, 2026 |website=Politico |language=en}}</ref> The early three-way polling was very narrow, usually with Cornyn and Paxton making the runoff and no candidate close to winning outright.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Bitonel |first=Marco |date=December 21, 2025 |title=Polling shows tight GOP Primary as Texas Senate Race heats up |url=https://www.fox7austin.com/election/polling-shows-tight-gop-primary-texas-senate-race-heats-up |access-date=January 12, 2026 |website=FOX 7 Austin |language=en-US}}</ref> While campaigning, all three tapped into [[Islamophobia|Islamophobic]] sentiment in their advertisements and actions, with Cornyn and Paxton having targeted the [[EPIC City, Texas|EPIC City]] development in [[Collin County, Texas|Collin County]] with investigations and lawsuits.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Birenbaum |first=Gabby |date=January 26, 2026 |title=Anti-Islam rhetoric takes over Texas GOP primaries |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/01/26/texas-republicans-sharia-law-anti-muslim-rhetoric/ |access-date=January 27, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Texas |first=Michael Lozano |title=Texas senator pushes for federal probe into EPIC City |url=https://spectrumlocalnews.com/tx/south-texas-el-paso/news/2025/04/21/texas-muslim-community-probe |access-date=January 27, 2026 |website=spectrumlocalnews.com |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Serrano |first=Alejandro |date=December 6, 2025 |title=Texas AG Ken Paxton sues EPIC to stop Muslim housing project |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2025/12/05/texas-ken-paxton-epic-city-lawsuit/ |access-date=January 27, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref>

Cornyn led Paxton narrowly in the primary election, garnering 42.0% of the vote to Paxton's 40.5%, while Hunt placed a distant third with 13.5% of the vote.<ref name="SoS primary results" /><ref name="NYT011">{{cite web |title=Texas Senate Primary 2026: Live Election Results |url=https://www.nytimes.com/interactive/2026/us/elections/results-texas-us-senate-primary.html |website=The New York Times |access-date=March 4, 2026 |date=March 3, 2026}}</ref> Support for Cornyn and Paxton was relatively evenly spread across the state, though Cornyn performed slightly better in urban areas, especially [[Dallas]] and [[Austin, Texas|Austin]], while Paxton performed slightly better in rural areas, particularly in [[East Texas]].<ref name=":5">{{Cite web |last1=Coleman |first1=J. Miles |last2=Kondik |first2=Kyle |date=March 4, 2026 |title=Cornyn Staying Alive is Enough to Keep Texas Senate as Likely Republican |url=https://centerforpolitics.org/crystalball/cornyn-staying-alive-is-enough-to-keep-texas-senate-as-likely-republican/ |access-date=March 12, 2026 |website=Sabato's Crystal Ball |language=en-US}}</ref> Hunt performed slightly better in regions which backed Paxton, suggesting his presence on the ballot hurt Cornyn more than Paxton. Even in Hunt's [[Texas's 38th congressional district|own congressional district]], he came in third with barely over 20% of the vote.<ref name=":6">{{Cite web |last=Coleman |first=J. Miles |date=March 12, 2026 |title=Getting Deeper into Texas and North Carolina's Primaries |url=https://centerforpolitics.org/crystalball/getting-deeper-into-texas-and-north-carolinas-primaries/ |access-date=March 12, 2026 |website=Sabato's Crystal Ball |language=en-US}}</ref>

In the days leading up to the March 3 primary, Paxton suggested he could win outright and avoid a runoff. However, Cornyn overperformed expectations in what ''[[The Texas Tribune]]'' described as a "better-than-expected showing". Rolando Garcia, a member of the [[Republican Party of Texas|State Republican Executive Committee]] who supported Hunt in the primary but planned to back Paxton in the runoff, said the result should alarm the Paxton campaign.<ref name="Svit">{{cite web |last1=Klibanoff |first1=Eleanor |last2=Svitek |first2=Patrick |last3=Downey |first3=Renzo |title=Five takeaways from the March 2026 Texas primaries |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/03/04/texas-primaries-2026-takeaways-senate-trump-cornyn-paxton-talarico-congress/ |website=The Texas Tribune |access-date=March 4, 2026 |date=March 4, 2026}}</ref> On March 5, ''[[The Atlantic]]'' reported that Trump advisers expected the president to endorse Cornyn in the runoff election after his unexpectedly strong performance in the first round of voting.<ref name="atlantic1">{{cite web |last1=Parker |first1=Ashley |title=Trump Is Expected to Endorse Cornyn |url=https://www.theatlantic.com/politics/2026/03/trump-cornyn-endorsement-texas/686232/ |website=The Atlantic |access-date=March 5, 2026 |language=en |date=March 4, 2026}}</ref>

=== Runoff ===

==== Campaign ====
Political observers described the runoff campaign between Paxton and Cornyn as "vile" and "brutal", featuring almost exclusively negative advertising from both sides.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Dallas Morning News Editorial Board |date=May 12, 2026 |title=The vile unreality of Ken Paxton, John Cornyn, Mayes Middleton and Chip Roy |url=https://www.dallasnews.com/opinion/editorials/article/vile-unreality-paxton-cornyn-middleton-roy-22253446.php |access-date=May 12, 2026 |website=Dallas News |language=en}}</ref><ref name=":8">{{Cite web |last=Guo |first=Kayla |date=May 14, 2026 |title=Cornyn and Paxton go scorched earth in Senate GOP runoff |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/05/14/texas-us-senate-gop-runoff-cornyn-paxton-attack-ads-spending/ |access-date=May 14, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> Cornyn repeatedly attacked Paxton's character, especially regarding alleged [[infidelity]] towards his wife, state senator [[Angela Paxton]], who had filed for divorce on "biblical grounds", and compared his ethics to that of a "strip club owner."<ref>{{Cite web |last=Morton |first=Joseph |date=May 8, 2026 |title=John Cornyn campaign has sharp Mother's Day message for Ken Paxton |url=https://www.dallasnews.com/news/politics/article/john-cornyn-sends-ken-paxton-mother-s-day-22249024.php |access-date=May 12, 2026 |website=Dallas News |language=en}}</ref><ref name=":7">{{Cite web |last=Zimmermann |first=David |date=May 8, 2026 |title=Cornyn compares Paxton's ethics to a strip club owner's |url=https://www.washingtonexaminer.com/news/campaigns/congressional/4561007/texas-senate-primary-cornyn-paxton-strip-club/ |access-date=May 12, 2026 |website=Washington Examiner |language=en-US}}</ref> He also highlighted the political scandals that have plagued Paxton's career, including his [[Ken Paxton#Texas House investigation and impeachment|2023 impeachment]] by the [[Texas House of Representatives]], framing him as corrupt and unfit for political office.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Solomon |first=Dan |date=May 11, 2026 |title=John Cornyn's Inexplicably Deep-Lore Campaign Against Ken Paxton |url=https://www.texasmonthly.com/news-politics/john-cornyn-obscure-campaign-ken-paxton/ |access-date=May 12, 2026 |website=The Texan}}</ref> Paxton continued to frame Cornyn as insufficiently loyal to Donald Trump.<ref name=":7" /><ref>{{Cite web |last=Dearman |first=Eleanor |date=April 24, 2026 |title=Sen. John Cornyn is 'not a Trump guy,' AG Ken Paxton tells Grapevine Republicans |url=https://www.star-telegram.com/news/politics-government/state-politics/article315516064.html |access-date=May 12, 2026 |website=[[Fort Worth Star-Telegram]]}}</ref> His office also drew scrutiny over a sharp increase in publicly-announced lawsuits made in his capacity as [[Texas Attorney General|attorney general]] since announcing his Senate run and especially in the months immediately before the March primary. A sizeable number of these actions focused on issues salient to conservative voters, including [[Immigration policy in Texas|immigration]], [[Islam]], [[DEI]], [[student protest]]s, and elections, drawing accusations that he used his office to energize his voter base and boost his campaign ahead of the runoff.<ref>{{Cite web|last1=Brooks Harper|first1=Karen|last2=Jankowski|first2=Philip |date=May 14, 2026 |title=As Cornyn-Paxton race heats up, AG's public investigations surge |url=https://www.dallasnews.com/news/politics/article/cornyn-paxton-senate-race-heats-up-ag-public-22253455.php |access-date=May 20, 2026 |website=Dallas News |language=en}}</ref>

The runoff significantly fractured both the Republican electorate and officeholders in the state, many of whom worried that the money spent and political attacks exchanged during the contest could benefit Democratic nominee [[James Talarico]]. Talarico won his primary outright, avoiding a runoff and allowing him to shift his focus to the general election early.<ref name=":8" /><ref>{{Cite web |last=Moritz |first=John C. |date=May 10, 2026 |title=From Hallmark to hardball: John Cornyn and Ken Paxton go scorched earth |url=https://www.statesman.com/news/columns/article/texas-senate-paxton-cornyn-republican-runoff-22249941.php |access-date=May 12, 2026 |website=Austin American-Statesman |language=en}}</ref> [[Lieutenant Governor of Texas|Lieutenant governor]] [[Dan Patrick (politician)|Dan Patrick]] even warned that a depressed Republican turnout could jeopardize Republican control of the Texas House, though most others dismissed that as extremely unlikely, with many Republicans still expressing confidence that both Paxton and Cornyn could win the general election.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Serrano |first=Alejandro |date=April 8, 2026 |title=Dan Patrick says GOP will have "tough time" holding Texas House |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/04/08/texas-house-dan-patrick-gop-majority-2026-midterms-cornyn-paxton/ |access-date=May 12, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Svitek |first=Patrick |date=April 30, 2026 |title='It's a mess': With no Trump endorsement in Texas Senate race, Cornyn and Paxton are locked in an expensive brawl |url=https://www.cnn.com/2026/04/30/politics/texas-senate-runoff-john-cornyn-ken-paxton-donald-trump |access-date=May 20, 2026 |website=CNN |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|last1=James |first1=Julia |last2=Jankowski |first2=Philip |date=May 4, 2026 |title=Texas House fight likely narrows to few districts despite chatter of shakeup this year |url=https://www.dallasnews.com/news/politics/article/control-texas-house-likely-narrows-districts-22230082.php |access-date=May 20, 2026 |website=Dallas News |language=en}}</ref>

Despite speculation following his first-place primary finish that Trump would endorse Cornyn, Trump did not endorse either candidate by the March 17 withdrawal deadline. Republicans had hoped the non-endorsed candidate would exit to avoid a costly runoff and allow the nominee to focus on the general election. However, Paxton refused to withdraw unless the Senate eliminated the [[filibuster]] to pass the [[SAVE America Act]]. Meanwhile, Cornyn, previously a staunch defender of the filibuster, signaled openness to reforming or bypassing it, as Trump indicated his endorsement could hinge on the bill's passage.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Birenbaum |first=Gabby |date=March 17, 2026 |title=Cornyn, Paxton locked in for runoff ballot after deadline |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/03/17/texas-senate-gop-primary-runoff-deadline-remove-name-from-ballot-cornyn-paxton/ |access-date=March 18, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Diamante |first=Reena |date=March 18, 2026 |title=Cornyn reconsiders Senate filibuster as he and Paxton stay in runoff race |url=https://spectrumlocalnews.com/tx/south-texas-el-paso/news/2026/03/18/texas-senate-gop-primary-runoff-filibuster- |access-date=March 18, 2026 |website=spectrumlocalnews.com |language=en}}</ref> Both candidates continued to court Trump's endorsement to consolidate support from the party, but Trump remained neutral in the race over two months after initially signaling his intention to endorse one of them.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Schwager |first=Adam |date=May 9, 2026 |title=Trump on endorsing Cornyn or Paxton in Texas Senate runoff: 'I'll make a decision' |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/nexstar_media_wire/5870665-trump-on-endorsing-cornyn-or-paxton-in-texas-senate-runoff-ill-make-a-decision/?tbref=hp |access-date=May 12, 2026 |website=The Hill}}</ref> This jockeying included policy-related moves as well as superficial ones, such as a bill introduced by Cornyn to designate [[U.S. Route 287]] as Interstate 47 to honor Trump.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Ardary |first=Steven |date=May 12, 2026 |title=Cornyn moves to honor Trump with Highway 287 renaming bill |url=https://www.fox26houston.com/news/cornyn-trump-interstate-highway-287 |access-date=May 14, 2026 |website=FOX 26 |language=en-US}}</ref>

On May 19, one week before the runoff, Trump announced his endorsement of Ken Paxton.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Bowman |first=Bridget |date=May 19, 2026 |title=Trump endorses Ken Paxton over Sen. John Cornyn ahead of Texas Republican Senate runoff |url=https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/trump-endorses-ken-paxton-sen-john-cornyn-ahead-texas-republican-senat-rcna345898 |access-date=May 19, 2026 |website=[[NBC News]] |language=en-US}}</ref> U.S. representative [[Wesley Hunt]], who finished third in the first round of the primary, also endorsed Paxton that day.<ref name="Hunt"/>

Following the runoff, Cornyn indicated tacit support for Paxton, saying he would vote for him and would back the Republican ticket but would not actively campaign for him.<ref name=Cornyn>{{Cite web |last=Touchberry |first=Ramsey |date=June 9, 2026 |title=Cornyn says he won't campaign for Paxton in Texas Senate race |url=https://www.washingtonexaminer.com/news/campaigns/congressional/4601401/cornyn-paxton-texas-senate-campaign-talarico/ |access-date=June 11, 2026 |website=Washington Examiner |language=en-US}}</ref>

Paxton defeated Cornyn with 64% of the runoff vote, becoming the first challenger to defeat an incumbent senator in a primary election in the state since [[Lloyd Bentsen]] defeated [[Ralph Yarborough]] in [[1970 United States Senate election in Texas|1970]].<ref name=":9">{{Cite web |last1=Martinez |first1=Alejandra |last2=Guo |first2=Kayla |last3=Birenbaum |first3=Gabby |date=May 27, 2026 |title=Ken Paxton defeats John Cornyn in Texas U.S. Senate GOP runoff |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/05/26/texas-john-cornyn-ken-paxton-us-senate-republican-primary-runoff/ |access-date=May 30, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> Cornyn became the second senator in the [[2026 United States Senate elections|2026 election cycle]] to lose renomination, after [[Bill Cassidy]] in [[2026 United States Senate election in Louisiana|Louisiana]]. His loss largely mirrored Cassidy's, performing best in the state's most Democratic-leaning areas, as well as wealthier suburban areas. Excluding sparsely populated [[Kenedy County, Texas|Kenedy County]], Cornyn only won [[Travis County, Texas|Travis County]], home to the state capital [[Austin, Texas|Austin]], a heavily left-leaning area, while narrowly losing [[Dallas County, Texas|Dallas County]], home to the wealthy [[Park Cities, Texas|Park Cities]] and [[Southern Methodist University]].<ref>{{Cite web |last1=Coleman |first1=J. Miles |last2=Kondik |first2=Kyle |date=May 27, 2026 |title=Texas Senate to Leans Republican Following Paxton Win; TX-35 Makes Same Move; Redistricting Updates |url=https://centerforpolitics.org/crystalball/texas-senate-to-leans-republican-following-paxton-win-tx-35-makes-same-move-redistricting-updates/ |access-date=May 30, 2026 |website=Sabato's Crystal Ball |language=en-US}}</ref> Paxton's runoff performance was the largest for a second-place finisher in a U.S. Senate primary in Texas, eclipsing [[Charles A. Culberson]]'s previous record in [[1916 United States Senate election in Texas|1916]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Ostermeier |first=Eric |date=May 27, 2026 |title=Ken Paxton and a Review of Texas US Senate Primary Runoffs |url=https://smartpolitics.lib.umn.edu/2026/05/27/ken-paxton-and-a-review-of-texas-us-senate-primary-runoffs/ |access-date=May 31, 2026 |website=Smart Politics |language=en-US}}</ref>

==Democratic primary==
[[File:Rep. Jasmine Crockett - 118th Congress (cropped 3x4).jpg|thumb|170x170px|[[U.S. Representative]] [[Jasmine Crockett]] finished second in the Democratic primary.]]

=== Background ===
Democrats, who have not won a statewide election in Texas since [[1994 Texas lieutenant gubernatorial election|1994]], see an opening in the state due to the bitter and divisive primary fight in the Republican side, a potential midterm backlash against the [[Second Trump presidency|Trump administration]], negative approval ratings for President Trump in Texas, as well as recent polling numbers showing competitive matchups.<ref>{{cite web | url=https://www.ktsm.com/news/texas-politics/state-of-texas-republicans-announce-campaigns-democrats-plot-strategy-for-2026/ | title=State of Texas: Republicans announce campaigns, Democrats plot strategy for 2026 | work=KTSM 9 News | date=June 28, 2025 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=June 27, 2025 |title=Democrats Allred, Castro, O'Rourke, Talarico meet to discuss 2026 options |url=https://www.dallasnews.com/news/politics/2025/06/26/democrats-allred-castro-orourke-talarico-meet-to-discuss-2026-options/ |access-date=June 29, 2025 |website=Dallas News |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=June 26, 2025 |title=Donald Trump's approval rating goes negative in Texas |url=https://www.newsweek.com/donald-trump-approval-rating-negative-texas-2091135 |access-date=June 29, 2025 |website=Newsweek |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Guo |first=Kayla |date=June 16, 2025 |title=Here are some of the Texas Democrats eyeing statewide runs in 2026 |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2025/06/16/texas-democrats-candidates-2026-midterms-senate-governor/ |access-date=June 29, 2025 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en}}</ref>

[[Colin Allred]], who unsuccessfully ran against incumbent Republican senator [[Ted Cruz]] in [[2024 United States Senate election in Texas|2024]], exited the race on December 8, 2025, which precipitated [[Jasmine Crockett]]'s entry into the race.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Fields |first=Ashleigh |date=December 9, 2025 |title=Allred says he dropped Senate bid after 'professional, friendly conversation' with Crockett  |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5640369-allred-crockett-texas-congressional-race/ |access-date=December 9, 2025 |website=The Hill |language=en}}</ref>

=== Campaign ===
[[File:¡Viva_Democracy!_Awesome_turnout_last_night_at_the_James_Talarico_Rally_at_The_Backyard.jpg|thumb|A Talarico rally at The Backyard in [[San Antonio]]]]

Crockett entered the race with high name recognition and strong polling numbers, but her launch on the last day to file meant she had limited time for campaigning statewide.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Guo |first=Kayla |date=December 12, 2025 |title=Crockett leads Talarico in first head-to-head Senate poll |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2025/12/12/texas-senate-democratic-primary-poll-jasmine-crockett-james-talarico/ |access-date=April 28, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> According to polling data in February 2026 prior to the primaries, Crockett originally had a favorability percentage of 23%, and an unfavorable percentage of 25% while Talarico came in at 18% favorable and 9% unfavorable.<ref>{{Cite web |date=February 23, 2026 |title=Jasmine Crockett Favorability (February 2026) |url=https://texaspolitics.utexas.edu/set/jasmine-crockett-favorability-february-2026 |access-date=April 28, 2026 |website=The Texas Politics Project |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=February 23, 2026 |title=James Talarico Favorability (February 2026) |url=https://texaspolitics.utexas.edu/set/james-talarico-favorability-february-2026 |access-date=April 28, 2026 |website=The Texas Politics Project |language=en}}</ref> She did not hire a [[campaign manager]], and focused on advertising over a staffed [[Canvassing|field operation]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Guo |first=Kayla |date=March 6, 2026 |title=Why Jasmine Crockett lost the Dem primary to James Talarico |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/03/06/jasmine-crockett-texas-james-talarico-democrat-us-senate-primary/ |access-date=March 22, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref>

Crockett and Talarico participated in a debate on January 24, 2026, hosted by the [[AFL-CIO]] in [[Georgetown, Texas|Georgetown]]. During the debate, both candidates largely aligned on policy and engaged in limited direct attacks. Crockett defended her "firebrand" style as necessary for the political moment, while Talarico emphasized a more measured approach and his perceived electability against Ken Paxton.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Mueller |first=Julia |date=January 24, 2026 |title=5 takeaways from the Texas Senate debate between Crockett, Talarico |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5704882-texas-senate-democrats-debate/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260126025423/https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5704882-texas-senate-democrats-debate/ |archive-date=January 26, 2026 |access-date=January 27, 2026 |work=The Hill |language=en-US}}</ref> Talarico later faced controversy after a social media influencer alleged he had referred to former rival Colin Allred as a "mediocre Black man" in comparison to Crockett in a private conversation; Talarico disputed the claim as a mischaracterization. Allred subsequently endorsed Crockett and criticized Talarico.<ref name="auto" /> The episode further exposed the racial tension between each candidate's base of support, with Crockett performing best among [[African Americans|Black]] voters, Democrats' strongest voter base, and Talarico performing best among [[White Americans|White]] and [[Hispanic and Latino Americans|Latino]] voters, demographics which hold far more [[swing vote]]rs.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Bowman |first=Bridget |date=February 5, 2026 |title=Viral dispute over 'mediocre' comment exposes racial divides in Democrats' Texas Senate primary |url=https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/viral-dispute-mediocre-comment-exposes-racial-divides-democrats-texas-rcna257284 |access-date=February 5, 2026 |website=NBC News |language=en}}</ref>

On February 16, Talarico was scheduled to appear on ''[[The Late Show with Stephen Colbert]]''. According to Colbert, however, [[CBS]] did not allow him to air the interview nor mention the cancellation on-air based on a recently revised interpretation of the [[Federal Communications Commission|FCC]]'s [[equal-time rule]], although he did discuss it during that night's episode in spite of FCC threats. Colbert later posted the interview to the show's [[YouTube]] page.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Moorman |first=Taijuan |title=Colbert slams CBS for canning interview with Trump critic |url=https://www.usatoday.com/story/entertainment/tv/2026/02/17/colbert-james-talarico-cbs-interview-trump/88715400007/ |access-date=February 17, 2026 |website=USA TODAY |language=en-US}}</ref> The interview gained millions of views online, while Talarico fundraised $2.5 million in the aftermath and increased his name recognition as [[early voting]] began ahead of the March 3 primary. Analysts largely viewed the campaign as one based on personality and electability rather than ideology.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Mueller |first=Julia |date=February 19, 2026 |title=Talarico gets burst of momentum from CBS controversy |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5744369-cbs-late-show-controversy/ |access-date=February 21, 2026 |website=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]]}}</ref>

After the primary, Crockett conceded to Talarico,<ref>{{Cite web |date=March 4, 2026|title=Crockett, after warning of disenfranchisement, concedes to Talarico in Texas Senate race|url=https://www.politico.com/news/2026/03/04/crockett-voting-confusion-dallas-texas-00811419|access-date=April 27, 2026|website=Politico|language=en}}</ref> announcing support for his candidacy, but she declined to actively campaign for him.<ref name="Rahman">{{cite web|last=Rahman|first=Yasira|date=March 5, 2026|title=Jasmine Crockett backs James Talarico after loss but won't 'make plans' to campaign for fellow Dem|url=https://news.meaww.com/jasmine-crockett-backs-james-talarico-after-loss-but-wont-make-plans-to-campaign-for-fellow-dem|website=MEAWW|access-date=May 31, 2026}}</ref>

'''Executive branch officials'''

{{Election box end}}
James Talarico won the Democratic primary with 52.4% of the vote.<ref name="primary-results-nyt">{{cite news|author1=NYT Election Results Team |title=Texas U.S. Senate Primary Election Results |url=https://www.nytimes.com/interactive/2026/us/elections/results-texas-us-senate-primary.html |access-date=March 4, 2026|work=[[The New York Times]] |date=March 4, 2026}}</ref> After a dispute concerning voting hours in [[Dallas County, Texas|Dallas]] and [[Williamson County, Texas|Williamson Counties]] on the night of the primary, Crockett conceded the race on March 4.<ref name="crockett-resign">{{cite news |last1=Howard |first1=Andrew |last2=Crampton |first2=Liz |title=Crockett, after warning of disenfranchisement, concedes to Talarico in Texas Senate race |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2026/03/04/crockett-voting-confusion-dallas-texas-00811419 |access-date=March 4, 2026 |work=Politico |date=March 4, 2026}}</ref> The dispute in Dallas County arose from the county Republican Party's decision not to use countywide voting centers, where voters can cast ballots at any location, on Election Day, as Texas law requires both parties to agree in order to implement them.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Votebeat |date=March 4, 2026 |title=Dallas County voters frustrated over precinct-based primary |url=https://dallasfreepress.com/west-dallas/dallas-county-precinct-voting-problems-jasmine-crockett-james-talarico-democrats-gop/ |access-date=March 17, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> On March 17, the Dallas County GOP announced it would use countywide voting centers for the runoff.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Contreras |first=Natalia |date=March 17, 2026 |title=Dallas GOP will OK countywide voting sites for May 26 runoff |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/03/17/dallas-county-republican-party-voting-sites-primary-runoff/ |access-date=March 17, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref>

Issues with a ballot scanning and tabulation machine in [[Newton County, Texas|Newton County]] led to the county clerk writing in a court filing that its Democratic statewide primary results, with Hassan winning the county, were "skewed" and that a recount was required, which eventually had to be done by hand due to the scanning issues persisting,<ref>{{Cite web |date=March 10, 2026 |title=Texas county starts election recount after "ballot coding error" leads to "skewed" results |url=https://abc13.com/post/texas-county-starts-election-recount-ballot-coding-error-leads-skewed-results/18697268/ |access-date=March 12, 2026 |website=ABC13 Houston |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=March 10, 2026 |title=Officials may have to have hand count votes after Newton County recount fails electronically |url=https://abc13.com/post/officials-may-have-hand-count-votes-newton-county-recount-fails-electronically/18700356/ |access-date=March 12, 2026 |website=ABC13 Houston |language=en}}</ref> with the revised results showing Crockett winning the county.

Democrats cast more primary votes in a midterm election than Republicans for the first time since [[2002 United States Senate election in Texas|2002]].<ref name=":6" /> The results largely mirrored those of the [[2020 United States Senate election in Texas#Democratic primary|2020 primary for this seat]], which also pitted a Black Democrat from Dallas ([[Royce West]]) against a White Democrat from the [[Austin, Texas|Austin]] area ([[M. J. Hegar]]). Crockett performed best among Black voters, winning by large margins in [[East Texas]], as well as by smaller margins in the urban centers of Dallas and [[Harris County, Texas|Harris]] counties. Talarico won the White and [[Hispanic and Latino Americans|Hispanic]] vote, dominating in the Austin metro area, and winning the state's remaining rural areas, including the heavily-Hispanic [[Rio Grande Valley]].<ref name=":5" />

== Libertarian convention ==
The [[Libertarian Party (United States)|Libertarian Party]] convention was held from April 10 to 12, 2026, in [[Abilene, Texas|Abilene]].<ref>{{Cite web |title=2026 Libertarian Party Convention |url=https://2026.lptexas.org/ |access-date=March 24, 2026 |website=2026 Convention |language=en}}</ref> Ted Brown, the party's U.S. senate nominee in [[2024 United States Senate election in Texas|2024]], secured the nomination with 83 votes to Daniel "Mark" Sims's 39.<ref name="BLib">{{cite web |title=United States Senate election in Texas, 2026 |url=https://ballotpedia.org/United_States_Senate_election_in_Texas,_2026 |website=Ballotpedia |access-date=April 17, 2026 |language=en |date=March 4, 2026}}</ref>
== General election ==

=== Campaign ===
Following the May Republican runoff, both nominees quickly pivoted to the general election.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Zurcher |first=Anthony |date=May 27, 2026 |title=Ken Paxton's win over John Cornyn in Texas primary may also help Democrats in Senate battle |url=https://www.bbc.com/news/articles/clypprglrlyo |access-date=June 11, 2026 |website=BBC News |language=en-GB}}</ref> Paxton's victory was welcomed by Democrats who viewed him as a weaker opponent, leading several nonpartisan forecasters to rate the race as more competitive.<ref name=":9" /> Talarico adopted a more aggressive strategy, highlighting allegations that led to Paxton's impeachment by fellow Republicans.<ref>{{Cite web |date=May 28, 2026 |title=Talarico targets Paxton's scandals in Texas Senate race, pivoting from his sunny primary message |url=https://www.pbs.org/newshour/politics/talarico-targets-paxtons-scandals-in-texas-senate-race-pivoting-from-his-sunny-primary-message |access-date=June 11, 2026 |website=PBS}}</ref> Paxton portrayed Talarico as an extremist, emphasizing [[culture war]] issues and [[gender]], while criticizing his statement that "[[God]] is [[non-binary]]" and questioning his [[masculinity]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Bowman |first=Bridget |date=May 27, 2026 |title=Ken Paxton defeats GOP Sen. John Cornyn in Texas primary runoff |url=https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/texas-runoff-primary-election-winner-paxton-trump-cornyn-rcna346552 |access-date=June 11, 2026 |website=NBC News |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Kurtzleben |first=Danielle |date=May 30, 2026 |title=What it means to be a man is a theme in Texas Senate race as Paxton attacks Talarico |url=https://www.npr.org/2026/05/30/nx-s1-5839468/texas-senate-talarico-paxton-gender-masculinity |access-date=June 11, 2026 |work=NPR |language=en}}</ref> Paxton also repeatedly called Talarico a vegan, claiming "you can't get elected as a vegan in Texas," prompting Talarico to release photos and videos of himself eating meat and other non-vegan foods at campaign events.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2026/may/27/ken-paxton/in-texas-senate-race-ken-paxton-falsely-claims-jam/|title= Democratic Texas U.S. Senate candidate James Talarico "is a vegan."|work=PolitiFact|date=May 26, 2026}}</ref> In June, Talarico criticized the Attorney General's office over its handling of a child sexual abuse case involving former attorney Adam Hoffman during Paxton's tenure.<ref name=":10">{{Cite news |last=McGaughy |first=Lauren |date=June 18, 2026 |title=James Talarico Attacks Ken Paxton Over Handling of Child Sex Abuse Case |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2026/06/18/us/talarico-paxton-child-sex-abuse-case.html |access-date=June 23, 2026 |work=The New York Times |language=en-US}}</ref> After a mistrial, Hoffman received a plea deal critics called "unusually lenient" and an "[[Jeffrey Epstein|Epstein]]-style sweetheart deal," as it did not require him to register as a sex offender.<ref>{{Cite web |last1=Sitz |first1=Alicia |last2=Carter |first2=Tatiana |date=June 8, 2026 |title=As Paxton heads to Senate race, critics revisit child sex abuse plea deal |url=https://www.newsnationnow.com/crime/paxton-senate-sex-abuse-plea-deal/ |website=NewsNation}}</ref> Talarico and John Cornyn had previously aired campaign ads criticizing Paxton over the case.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Powell |first=Adam |title=Cornyn keeps up attacks on Paxton in Texas US Senate runoff |url=https://www.elpasotimes.com/story/news/politics/elections/2026/05/07/cornyn-targets-paxton-over-plea-deal-for-sex-abuser-in-texas-us-senate-race/89977335007/ |access-date=June 23, 2026 |website=El Paso Times |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Guo |first=Kayla |date=May 27, 2026 |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/05/27/james-talarico-ken-paxton-launch-attack-ads-in-texas-u-s-senate-race/ |title=Tala-freak-o vs. Ken the Criminal: Texas candidates sharpen attacks as U.S. Senate race locks in |work=[[The Texas Tribune]] |access-date=September 20, 2026}}</ref>

Both Talarico and Paxton have faced challenges unifying their parties after the primaries, with limited support from former rivals exposing weaknesses in their coalitions.<ref name=":11">{{Cite web |last=Guo |first=Kayla |date=June 24, 2026 |title=Cornyn, Crockett loom large in Senate race after primary losses |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/06/24/john-cornyn-jasmine-crockett-talarico-paxton-texas-senate/ |access-date=July 9, 2026 |website=[[The Texas Tribune]] |language=en-US}}</ref> Talarico has sought to bolster support among Black voters after defeating Jasmine Crockett, who skipped the state Democratic convention and declined to commit to campaigning for him, raising concerns about Black turnout.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Guo |first=Kayla |date=June 27, 2026 |title=Talarico looks for momentum with Black voters at Dem convention |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/06/27/texas-democratic-convention-james-talarico-black-voters-jasmine-crockett/ |access-date=July 9, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> Paxton has struggled with moderate Republicans who backed John Cornyn, with polls showing some Cornyn voters supporting Talarico.<ref name=":11" /> Cornyn has also declined to fundraise for Paxton, instead focusing on competitive [[2026 United States Senate elections|Senate races in other states]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Birenbaum |first=Gabby |date=August 28, 2026 |title=Why John Cornyn won't be fundraising in Texas' U.S. Senate race |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/08/28/john-cornyn-ken-paxton-texas-fundraising-talarico-senate-race/ |access-date=August 28, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> 

In part to rally support behind candidates such as Paxton, the Republican Party hosted its first ever [[2026 Republican National Convention|midterm national convention]] in [[Dallas]] on September 9 and 10.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Ardary |first=Steven |date=September 2, 2026 |title=Trump says Dallas GOP midterm convention will be 'big stuff' |url=https://www.fox4news.com/news/trump-dallas-gop-midterm-convention-paxton-talarico |access-date=September 4, 2026 |website=FOX Local |publisher=FOX 4 News Dallas-Fort Worth |language=en-US}}</ref> After the midterm convention, Paxton said in a private conversation that the convention had a negative effect for the Republican Party.<ref>{{cite web|url=https://www.mediaite.com/media/news/bombshell-leaked-audio-shows-ken-paxton-dissing-trumps-convention-blaming-it-for-his-bad-polls-everybodys-numbers-dropped/|title=Bombshell Leaked Audio Shows Ken Paxton Dissing Trump's Convention — Blaming It for His Bad Polls: 'Everybody's Numbers Dropped'|work=Mediaite|date=September 30, 2026|last=Durante |first=Tom|access-date =September 30, 2026}}</ref>

On July 16, the Paxton and Talarico campaigns announced negotiations for up to three general election debates.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Whitely |first=Jason |date=July 16, 2026 |title=Talarico says yes to three debates. Paxton's response: 'Of course we are going to debate.' |url=https://www.wfaa.com/article/news/local/talarico-says-yes-three-debates-paxton/287-4b91cce7-20e7-49af-a119-72efe369990b |website=WFAA}}</ref> If held, they would mark Paxton's first debates since becoming a statewide elected official.<ref>{{Cite news |last=McGaughy |first=Lauren |date=July 16, 2026 |title=Paxton Agrees to Debate Talarico in Texas Senate Race |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2026/07/16/us/paxton-talarico-debate-texas-senate.html |access-date=July 20, 2026 |work=The New York Times |language=en-US |issn=0362-4331}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Birenbaum |first=Gabby |date=July 16, 2026 |title=Ken Paxton says he'll debate James Talarico in Texas Senate race |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/07/16/texas-ken-paxton-james-talarico-debate-senate-race-2026/ |access-date=July 20, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Barragán |first=James |date=February 24, 2022 |title=Debate between Texas GOP attorney general candidates got heated, even though incumbent Ken Paxton wasn't there |url=https://www.texastribune.org/?p=109993 |access-date=July 20, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> By September, Talarico had accepted a fourth invitation to a debate as Paxton had remained noncommittal, but insisted that they were still negotiating and expected to participate in at least one debate before the election.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Bugenhagen |first=Faith |date=September 2, 2026 |title=Talarico accepts debate invitation; Paxton criticizes event but doesn't commit |url=https://www.statesman.com/news/politics/state/article/talarico-paxton-cnn-texas-senate-debate-22414048.php |access-date=September 18, 2026 |website=Austin American-Statesman |language=en}}</ref> Responding to a challenge issued by Talarico to appear on the channel,<ref>{{Cite web |last=Guo|first=Kayla |date=September 24, 2026 |title=James Talarico challenges Ken Paxton to Fox News debate |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/09/24/james-talarico-tribfest-2026/ |access-date=October 5, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> a fifth debate invitation was issued by Fox News.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Murray |first=Casey |date=September 29, 2026 |title=Fox News will host Texas Senate debate if Ken Paxton agrees to face off|url=https://www.houstonchronicle.com/politics/texas/article/paxton-talarico-debate-22455301.php |access-date=October 5, 2026 |website=Houston Chronicle |language=en}}</ref> In early October, president Donald Trump and several other prominent Republicans called on Paxton to debate Talarico.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Rahman |first=Khaleda |date=October 9, 2026 |title=Ken Paxton Avoided Debates for Years. Now He May Have No Choice |url=https://www.newsweek.com/ken-paxton-avoided-debates-years-may-have-no-choice-12540874 |access-date=October 11, 2026 |website=Newsweek |language=en}}</ref>

=== Election administration ===
The election is taking place amidst a broader push by the Trump administration to restrict [[Postal voting in the United States|mail-in voting]], although these efforts are currently mired in court proceedings.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Gilani |first=Haajrah |date=September 2, 2026 |title=What the US Postal Service's effort to reshape mail-in voting means for Texas |url=https://www.expressnews.com/politics/article/usps-mail-voting-texas-22412931.php |access-date=September 4, 2026 |website=San Antonio Express-News |language=en}}</ref> On September 1, commissioners in [[Tarrant County]], Texas's third-largest county, voted to close roughly a third of its polling locations, a year after cutting early-voting sites in half, citing cost savings at low-turnout locations. Critics warned the closures could increase travel times and suppress turnout.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Zaru |first=Deena |title=Amid public outcry, Tarrant County, Texas cuts polling locations ahead of 2026 midterm elections |url=https://abcnews.com/Politics/amid-public-outcry-tarrant-county-texas-cuts-polling/story?id=136116581 |work=ABC News |date=September 1, 2026 |access-date=September 2, 2026}}</ref> Later that month, [[Votebeat]] reported that the [[Texas Department of Public Safety|Department of Public Safety]] had failed to distribute nearly 200,000 voter registrations to counties over the course of the past year due to a technical error, sparking a bipartisan outcry.<ref name=":2" /> By the beginning of October, the reported size of the backlog had neared 600,000 registrations.<ref>{{Cite web |last1=McManus |first1=Tracey |last2=Tong |first2=William |date=October 2, 2026 |title=Map: Texas DPS voter registrations backlog tracker |url=https://www.dallasnews.com/projects/2026/texas-voter-registration-backlog-tracker/ |access-date=October 4, 2026 |website=[[The Dallas Morning News]] |language=en}}</ref> The backlog was sent to counties all at once, with many of the state's urban counties receiving tens of thousands one month before [[early voting]] was set to begin.<ref name=":2">{{Cite web |last1=DeGuzman |first1=Colleen |last2=Contreras |first2=Natalia |date=September 24, 2026 |title=GOP official: Texas should have caught voter registration error |url=https://www.votebeat.org/texas/2026/06/24/team-voter-registration-system-civix-secretary-of-state/ |access-date=September 29, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> The state agreed to reimburse counties for the [[overtime]] pay incurred processing the glut of applications.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Behrndt |first=Rachel |date=September 29, 2026 |title=State agrees to pay counties as they work to clear voter registration backlog |url=https://www.wfaa.com/article/news/politics/elections/texas-voter-registration-back-log-funding-glitch-error-pays/287-c61b041d-f622-498d-b971-33838bb88182 |access-date=September 29, 2026 |work=WFAA}}</ref> Observers and election experts highlighted the debacle as an example of the difficulties involved in registering to vote in Texas, one of the only states in the country to not offer universal online voter registration.<ref>{{Cite web |last1=DeGuzman |first1=Colleen |last2=Contreras |first2=Natalia |date=September 26, 2026 |title=Texas' delayed voter applications highlight registration woes |url=https://www.votebeat.org/texas/2026/09/26/voter-registration-delay-backlog-online-dps-error/ |access-date=October 4, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref>

===Fundraising===
Talarico has considerably outraised Paxton throughout the campaign. Due to the high national profile of the race, both candidates, especially Talarico have received significant contributions from voters across the country. Within Texas, Talarico's donation base is concentrated in his home city of [[Austin, Texas|Austin]] and the state's other urban areas, while Paxton's is in [[Dallas]].<ref>{{Cite web |last1=Quinton |first1=Phoebe |last2=Moritz |first2=John C. |date=September 29, 2026 |title=The money behind Talarico and Paxton's Senate race, mapped by ZIP code |url=https://www.dallasnews.com/projects/2026/talarico-paxton-donations-map/ |access-date=October 1, 2026 |website=Dallas Morning News |language=en}}</ref> Despite Talarico's individual fundraising advantage, however, leading into the fall campaign season, and particularly after the [[2026 Republican National Convention|Republican National Convention]] in Dallas, Paxton has seen a massive infusion of [[political action committee]] and political party spending on his behalf to try to buoy his sagging polling numbers.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Howard |first=Andrew |date=September 11, 2026 |title=Republicans just opened the floodgates on spending for Ken Paxton |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2026/09/11/gop-money-texas-paxton-trump-01072972 |access-date=September 28, 2026|website=[[Politico]] |language=en}}</ref> The [[Senate Leadership Fund]] alone committed over $100 million to support Paxton, far exceeding Talarico's individual fundraising numbers, though a remarkably high investment to defend a seat in a state Republicans have not lost in over 30 years.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Birenbaum |first=Gabby |date=September 23, 2026 |title=Senate Republicans increase ad buy for Ken Paxton to $100M |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/09/22/texas-paxton-ads-100-million-senate-race-talarico/ |access-date=September 28, 2026 |website=The Texas Tribune |language=en-US}}</ref> [[Elon Musk]] also donated $10 million to Lone Star Liberty PAC, a group supporting Ken Paxton.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Elon Musk quietly becomes biggest known donor in midterm contests as he tries to save Republicans |website=[[AP News]] |url=https://apnews.com/article/elon-musk-midterms-republicans-donors-c06efc774a2e7c5d345e0d46641eb510}}</ref> The [[Supreme Court of the United States|Supreme Court]] over the summer additionally cleared the way for political parties themselves to freely coordinate advertising spending with candidates at the candidates' reduced rate, handing Republicans a monetary advantage across the country due to the Democratic Party lagging behind the Republicans in party fundraising.<ref>{{Cite web |last1=Doherty |first1=Erin |last2=Kashinsky |first2=Lisa |last3=Piper |first3=Jessica |last4=Howard |first4=Andrew |title=The Supreme Court just made the DNC's fundraising woes a much bigger problem|url=https://www.politico.com/news/2026/06/30/the-supreme-court-just-made-the-dncs-fundraising-woes-a-much-bigger-problem-00983172 |access-date=September 28, 2026 |website=Politico |language=en}}</ref> The Talarico campaign raised $64 million in the third quarter of 2026, the largest quarterly total by a U.S. Senate candidate ever, surpassing the $57 million record that had been set by [[Jaime Harrison]] in the [[2020 United States Senate election in South Carolina|2020 South Carolina senate race]].<ref>{{Cite web |date=October 15, 2026 |title=James Talarico announces record-breaking $64M haul |url=https://www.politico.com/live-updates/2026/10/15/congress/james-talarico-64-million-fundraising-record-01119808 |access-date=October 15, 2026 |website=[[Politico]] |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Birenbaum |first=Gabby |date=October 15, 2026 |title=James Talarico raises record $64 million in third quarter |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2026/10/15/james-talarico-64-million-fundraising-texas-senate/ |access-date=October 15, 2026 |work=[[The Texas Tribune]] |language=en-US}}</ref>
